Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well, Doctor, they say I’m here because of the project. The house project. My wife, my kids—they’re all up in arms because I used our savings. But you have to understand, it wasn't just throwing money away, I was buying essentials. Wood, lights, display stuff... a lot of lights, actually, because the illumination is key. I had to get it right. I was working on it all day, and yes, I kept pushing the work well after dark, out there with the floodlights on, hammering and wiring, because the vision was so clear. It was so perfectly clear to me. I felt like... I knew that if I stopped, even for a few hours to sleep, the whole house—and not just our house, but the whole block—would lose its chance. It’s like a window of opportunity, you know? A spiritual or cosmic window, and if you don't build it when the energy is there, it's gone forever. They don't see it. They just see the bank account and the noise at 2 AM, but they don't see what I was trying to give them.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Energetic? Doc, it’s like... it’s like I’ve been plugged straight into the main breaker of the universe. I mean, my wife, she bought me this big brown recliner a few years back when I stopped working. She expected me to just... I don't know, sit there and watch the television, waste away like the other old men on our street. But I can’t do it. I can’t just sit in the chair anymore. My body won't let me, the energy just hums right through my veins. 

I’m up and I'm moving constantly, from the minute the sun comes up—well, even before that, really, in the dead of night. I’ll be out in the garden, checking the perimeter, and then suddenly I know I need to be in the darkroom down in the basement to check the chemical trays, so I'm down there, and then straight out to the front porch. Just moving, moving, moving. I've been carrying tools with me everywhere, heavy tools, the wrenches and the wire cutters and the hammers, just hauling them from room to room. And I'm fixing things before anyone even asks. Before they even know it's broken! 

Because I see what needs doing before they do. That's the insight you're talking about. My wife will walk into the hallway and I’ve already taken the closet door off the hinges because it wasn't swinging in harmony with the energy of the house. It was blocking the flow. She doesn't understand the vision it takes to see that, she just yells about the mess and asks why I haven't slept. But how can I sleep? Over the top? No, no, no, you're not hearing me, Doctor. Over the top would be... well, over the top would be tearing the whole roof off, which, believe me, crossed my mind when I saw how the shingles were misaligned with the lunar cycle, but I didn't do that. I showed restraint! I just bought the materials we needed. 

And my wife, she says to me, "Frank, you spent three thousand dollars at Home Depot in two days," and I look at her and I say, "Martha, what is three thousand dollars compared to the structural integrity of our spiritual dwelling?" I mean, really, think about it. The bank account is just numbers on a screen, just little digital blips that don't mean a thing when the grid goes down or when the cosmic alignment shifts, which it is doing, by the way. You can feel it in the air, a sort of static cling on everything. 

So, no, I don't think it was unnecessary. I think it was the most necessary thing I've done in my entire seventy-three years on this earth. When I was out there at 3 AM with the floodlights, yes, the neighbors complained. Old Mrs. Gable next door, she came out in her robe, waving her arms, yelling about the noise of the circular saw. But I told her, I said, "Mrs. Gable, you will thank me when the vibrations settle!" She didn't listen, of course. People don't. They just want their quiet little lives, they want to sleep through the revolution, sleep through the awakening. 

But I can't sleep through it. I have to prepare the space. Every nail I drove was deliberate. Every wire I ran was essential. If I had stopped, if I had said, "Oh, maybe I shouldn't spend this money, maybe I should go to bed," the whole thing would have collapsed. The energy would have soured. So, to answer your question, no, I don't look back and think it was too much. I look back and think, if anything, I didn't have enough time to finish before they dragged me in here.

A week? Oh, Doc, a week is nothing. A week is just a drop in the bucket. No, this... this has been going on for about three months now. Three whole months since Martha decided we needed to remodel the kitchen. That was the catalyst, you see. The contractors came in, they started tearing down the drywall, pulling up the linoleum, and the whole house got turned upside down. The dust, the noise, the disruption of the natural pathways in the home—it shifted everything. 

And once they opened up those walls, I could see the framework, the actual bones of the house, and I realized how flawed it all was. How completely out of sync the original builders were with the magnetic fields. So that was three months ago, right around the end of September, I think. And from that very first day they started swinging their sledgehammers, I knew I had to step in. I had to take over. I couldn't just let them put up new cabinets and pretend the underlying energy wasn't completely stagnant. 

So it's been three months of constant, necessary vigilance. Ninety days, give or take, of me waking up before dawn, getting the materials, working alongside them—well, working around them, really, because they didn't understand what I was doing with the copper wiring and the quartz crystals I was embedding in the foundation. They actually tried to tell me to stop! Can you imagine? Some guy making twenty bucks an hour trying to tell me about structural integrity when he doesn't even know which way is true north. So, yes, it’s been about three months since everything got turned upside down and I had to start fixing it. A full week? If I had only worked for a week, the whole place would have collapsed in on itself by now.

My body won't let me, the energy just hums right through my veins. \n\nI’m up and I'm moving constantly, from the minute the sun comes up—well, even before that, really, in the dead of night. I’ll be out in the garden, checking the perimeter, and then suddenly I know I need to be in the darkroom down in the basement to check the chemical trays, so I'm down there, and then straight out to the front porch. Just moving, moving, moving. I've been carrying tools with me everywhere, heavy tools, the wrenches and the wire cutters and the hammers, just hauling them from room to room. And I'm fixing things before anyone even asks. Before they even know it's broken! \n\nBecause I see what needs doing before they do. That's the insight you're talking about. My wife will walk into the hallway and I’ve already taken the closet door off the hinges because it wasn't swinging in harmony with the energy of the house. It was blocking the flow. She doesn't understand the vision it takes to see that, she just yells about the mess and asks why I haven't slept. But how can I sleep? I don't have time to sleep or sit around in some recliner when there's that much work to be done, when I can literally feel the house vibrating, telling me what needs to be fixed next. I just have to keep moving, you see? I have to keep the momentum going.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when you realized that your actions, like working all night or spending so much on the project, might have been over the top or not entirely necessary?\n\nPatient: Over the top? No, no, no, you're not hearing me, Doctor. Over the top would be... well, over the top would be tearing the whole roof off, which, believe me, crossed my mind when I saw how the shingles were misaligned with the lunar cycle, but I didn't do that. I showed restraint! I just bought the materials we needed. \n\nAnd my wife, she says to me, \"Frank, you spent three thousand dollars at Home Depot in two days,\" and I look at her and I say, \"Martha, what is three thousand dollars compared to the structural integrity of our spiritual dwelling?\" I mean, really, think about it. The bank account is just numbers on a screen, just little digital blips that don't mean a thing when the grid goes down or when the cosmic alignment shifts, which it is doing, by the way. You can feel it in the air, a sort of static cling on everything. \n\nSo, no, I don't think it was unnecessary. I think it was the most necessary thing I've done in my entire seventy-three years on this earth. When I was out there at 3 AM with the floodlights, yes, the neighbors complained. Old Mrs. Gable next door, she came out in her robe, waving her arms, yelling about the noise of the circular saw. But I told her, I said, \"Mrs. Gable, you will thank me when the vibrations settle!\" She didn't listen, of course. People don't. They just want their quiet little lives, they want to sleep through the revolution, sleep through the awakening. \n\nBut I can't sleep through it. I have to prepare the space. Every nail I drove was deliberate. Every wire I ran was essential. I look back and think, if anything, I didn't have enough time to finish before they dragged me in here.\n\nDoctor: Doctor: When you were working on the house project so intensely, how long did that period last? Was it for at least a full week straight?\n\nPatient: A week? Oh, Doc, a week is nothing. A week is just a drop in the bucket. No, this... this has been going on for about three months now. Three whole months since Martha decided we needed to remodel the kitchen. That was the catalyst, you see. The contractors came in, they started tearing down the drywall, pulling up the linoleum, and the whole house got turned upside down. The dust, the noise, the disruption of the natural pathways in the home—it shifted everything. \n\nAnd once they opened up those walls, I could see the framework, the actual bones of the house, and I realized how flawed it all was. How completely out of sync the original builders were with the magnetic fields. So that was three months ago, right around the end of September, I think. And from that very first day they started swinging their sledgehammers, I knew I had to step in. I had to take over. I couldn't just let them put up new cabinets and pretend the underlying energy wasn't completely stagnant. \n\nSo it's been three months of constant, necessary vigilance. Ninety days, give or take, of me waking up before dawn, getting the materials, working alongside them—well, working around them, really, because they didn't understand what I was doing with the copper wiring and the quartz crystals I was embedding in the foundation. They actually tried to tell me to stop! Can you imagine? Some guy making twenty bucks an hour trying to tell me about structural integrity when he doesn't even know which way is true north. So, yes, it’s been about three months since everything got turned upside down and I had to start fixing it. A full week? If I had only worked for a week, the whole place would have collapsed in on itself by now.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. F31.3 (Bipolar Disorder, current episode manic without psychotic features) is considered due to the persistent manic-like behaviors. F31.88 (Other specified bipolar disorder) is added because the presentation may not fully meet criteria for a manic episode but still involves clinically significant hypomanic-like symptoms with functional impairment. F23.80 (Other specified schizophrenia spectrum and other psychotic disorders) is considered due to the possible presence of non-bizarre delusions related to cosmic alignment and spiritual dwelling, though not clearly persecutory or referential. Just moving, moving, moving. I've been carrying tools with me everywhere, heavy tools, the wrenches and the wire cutters and the hammers, just hauling them from room to room. And I'm fixing things before anyone even asks. Before they even know it's broken! \n\nBecause I see what needs doing before they do. That's the insight you're talking about. My wife will walk into the hallway and I’ve already taken the closet door off the hinges because it wasn't swinging in harmony with the energy of the house. It was blocking the flow. She doesn't understand the vision it takes to see that, she just yells about the mess and asks why I haven't slept. But how can I sleep? I don't have time to sleep or sit around in some recliner when there's that much work to be done, when I can literally feel the house vibrating, telling me what needs to be fixed next. I just have to keep moving, you see? I have to keep the momentum going.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when you realized that your actions, like working all night or spending so much on the project, might have been over the top or not entirely necessary?\n\nPatient: Over the top? No, no, no, you're not hearing me, Doctor. Over the top would be... well, over the top would be tearing the whole roof off, which, believe me, crossed my mind when I saw how the shingles were misaligned with the lunar cycle, but I didn't do that. I showed restraint! I just bought the materials we needed. \n\nAnd my wife, she says to me, \"Frank, you spent three thousand dollars at Home Depot in two days,\" and I look at her and I say, \"Martha, what is three thousand dollars compared to the structural integrity of our spiritual dwelling?\" I mean, really, think about it. The bank account is just numbers on a screen, just little digital blips that don't mean a thing when the grid goes down or when the cosmic alignment shifts, which it is doing, by the way. You can feel it in the air, a sort of static cling on everything. \n\nSo, no, I don't think it was unnecessary. I think it was the most necessary thing I've done in my entire seventy-three years on this earth. When I was out there at 3 AM with the floodlights, yes, the neighbors complained. Old Mrs. Gable next door, she came out in her robe, waving her arms, yelling about the noise of the circular saw. But I told her, I said, \"Mrs. Gable, you will thank me when the vibrations settle!\" She didn't listen, of course. People don't. They just want their quiet little lives, they want to sleep through the revolution, sleep through the awakening. \n\nBut I can't sleep through it. I have to prepare the space. Every nail I drove was deliberate. Every wire I ran was essential. If I had stopped, if I had said, \"Oh, maybe I shouldn't spend this money, maybe I should go to bed,\" the whole thing would have collapsed. The energy would have soured. So, to answer your question, no, I don't look back and think it was too much. I look back and think, if anything, I didn't have enough time to finish before they dragged me in here.\n\nDoctor: Doctor: When you were working on the house project so intensely, how long did that period last? Was it for at least a full week straight?\n\nPatient: A week? Oh, Doc, a week is nothing. A week is just a drop in the bucket. No, this... this has been going on for about three months now. Three whole months since Martha decided we needed to remodel the kitchen. That was the catalyst, you see. The contractors came in, they started tearing down the drywall, pulling up the linoleum, and the whole house got turned upside down. The dust, the noise, the disruption of the natural pathways in the home—it shifted everything. \n\nAnd once they opened up those walls, I could see the framework, the actual bones of the house, and I realized how flawed it all was. How completely out of sync the original builders were with the magnetic fields. So that was three months ago, right around the end of September, I think. And from that very first day they started swinging their sledgehammers, I knew I had to step in. I had to take over. I couldn't just let them put up new cabinets and pretend the underlying energy wasn't completely stagnant. \n\nSo it's been three months of constant, necessary vigilance. Ninety days, give or take, of me waking up before dawn, getting the materials, working alongside them—well, working around them, really, because they didn't understand what I was doing with the copper wiring and the quartz crystals I was embedding in the foundation. They actually tried to tell me to stop! Can you imagine? Some guy making twenty bucks an hour trying to tell me about structural integrity when he doesn't even know which way is true north. So, yes, it’s been about three months since everything got turned upside down and I had to start fixing it. A full week? If I had only worked for a week, the whole place would have collapsed in on itself by now.\n\n[Last Question]\nDoctor: In the time since this house project and all this activity, have you had a period where you felt down, depressed, or lost interest in things for several days or more?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Male / 73 / not_in_workforce / 9th_12th_no_diploma\n- severity: Severe\n\n[Targeted Info]\nNone\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Verbose patient.\nA verbose patient may\n1) provide excessively detailed responses when describing their symptoms and experiences, even when the details are directly relevant,\n2) elaborate extensively on the background, events, thoughts, feelings, and personal experiences surrounding their symptoms, and\n3) demonstrate difficulty allowing the doctor to guide the interview or move on to another clinical topic.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Down? Depressed? Doc, I don't... I really don't think you're grasping the sheer volume of purpose that's running through me right now. How could I possibly feel down? Down is for people who are disconnected. It’s for people who don't have a mission. Like... well, like my brother-in-law, Gary. Gary sits around all day in his undershirt watching golf on the television, just looking like a deflated balloon. Now *that* is depressed. 

But me? No, no, no, absolutely not. I haven't lost interest in a single thing! If anything, I am profoundly, deeply interested in *everything*. Every grain of wood in that house, every single millimeter of copper piping, the way the morning light hits the dust motes when I'm tearing up the floorboards... it all means something. It all has a frequency. 

There hasn't been a single day—not a day, not an hour, not even a minute—where I felt sad or low or whatever word you want to put on it. You can't be sad when you're the only one who can see how the universe is stitching itself together right there in your own hallway! The momentum, it just carries you. It's like a freight train, a freight train of pure energy. The very concept of being depressed is just... it's alien to me now. Completely alien. 

Now, Martha, she gets down. She cries about the drywall dust on her good rugs, she sits at the kitchen table and complains about the noise and the bank statements. But I tell her, I say, "Martha, you're looking at the dirt, you're not looking at the cathedral we're building!" You see? I don't have the luxury of feeling down. There’s just no room for it in my head or my body. None at all. Just pure, unbroken focus.

No, no, no, you're not hearing me, Doctor. Over the top would be... well, over the top would be tearing the whole roof off, which, believe me, crossed my mind when I saw how the shingles were misaligned with the lunar cycle, but I didn't do that. I showed restraint! I just bought the materials we needed. \n\nAnd my wife, she says to me, \"Frank, you spent three thousand dollars at Home Depot in two days,\" and I look at her and I say, \"Martha, what is three thousand dollars compared to the structural integrity of our spiritual dwelling?\" I mean, really, think about it. The bank account is just numbers on a screen, just little digital blips that don't mean a thing when the grid goes down or when the cosmic alignment shifts, which it is doing, by the way. You can feel it in the air, a sort of static cling on everything. \n\nSo, no, I don't think it was unnecessary. I think it was the most necessary thing I've done in my entire seventy-three years on this earth. When I was out there at 3 AM with the floodlights, yes, the neighbors complained. Old Mrs. Gable next door, she came out in her robe, waving her arms, yelling about the noise of the circular saw. But I told her, I said, \"Mrs. Gable, you will thank me when the vibrations settle!\" She didn't listen, of course. People don't. They just want their quiet little lives, they want to sleep through the revolution, sleep through the awakening. \n\nBut I can't sleep through it. I have to prepare the space. Every nail I drove was deliberate. Every wire I ran was essential. If I had stopped, if I had said, \"Oh, maybe I shouldn't spend this money, maybe I should go to bed,\" the whole thing would have collapsed. The energy would have soured. I look back and think, if anything, I didn't have enough time to finish before they dragged me in here.\n\nDoctor: Doctor: When you were working on the house project so intensely, how long did that period last? Was it for at least a full week straight?\n\nPatient: A week? Oh, Doc, a week is nothing. A week is just a drop in the bucket. No, this... this has been going on for about three months now. Three whole months since Martha decided we needed to remodel the kitchen. That was the catalyst, you see. The contractors came in, they started tearing down the drywall, pulling up the linoleum, and the whole house got turned upside down. The dust, the noise, the disruption of the natural pathways in the home—it shifted everything. \n\nAnd once they opened up those walls, I could see the framework, the actual bones of the house, and I realized how flawed it all was. How completely out of sync the original builders were with the magnetic fields. So that was three months ago, right around the end of September, I think. And from that very first day they started swinging their sledgehammers, I knew I had to step in. I had to take over. I couldn't just let them put up new cabinets and pretend the underlying energy wasn't completely stagnant. \n\nSo it's been three months of constant, necessary vigilance. Ninety days, give or take, of me waking up before dawn, getting the materials, working alongside them—well, working around them, really, because they didn't understand what I was doing with the copper wiring and the quartz crystals I was embedding in the foundation. They actually tried to tell me to stop! Can you imagine? Some guy making twenty bucks an hour trying to tell me about structural integrity when he doesn't even know which way is true north. So, yes, it’s been about three months since everything got turned upside down and I had to start fixing it. A full week? If I had only worked for a week, the whole place would have collapsed in on itself by now.\n\nDoctor: Doctor: In the time since this house project and all this activity, have you had a period where you felt down, depressed, or lost interest in things for several days or more?\n\nPatient: Down? Depressed? Doc, I don't... I really don't think you're grasping the sheer volume of purpose that's running through me right now. How could I possibly feel down? Down is for people who are disconnected. It’s for people who don't have a mission. Like... well, like my brother-in-law, Gary. Gary sits around all day in his undershirt watching golf on the television, just looking like a deflated balloon. Now *that* is depressed. \n\nBut me? No, no, no, absolutely not. I haven't lost interest in a single thing! If anything, I am profoundly, deeply interested in *everything*. Every grain of wood in that house, every single millimeter of copper piping, the way the morning light hits the dust motes when I'm tearing up the floorboards... it all means something. It all has a frequency. \n\nThere hasn't been a single day—not a day, not an hour, not even a minute—where I felt sad or low or whatever word you want to put on it. You can't be sad when you're the only one who can see how the universe is stitching itself together right there in your own hallway! The momentum, it just carries you. It's like a freight train, a freight train of pure energy. The very concept of being depressed is just... it's alien to me now. Completely alien. \n\nNow, Martha, she gets down. She cries about the drywall dust on her good rugs, she sits at the kitchen table and complains about the noise and the bank statements. But I tell her, I say, \"Martha, you're looking at the dirt, you're not looking at the cathedral we're building!\" You see? I don't have the luxury of feeling down. There’s just no room for it in my head or my body. None at all. Just pure, unbroken focus.\n\n[Last Question]\nHave you ever had a period like this before, where you felt extremely energetic and driven to work on big projects, maybe even so intense that someone suggested you needed help or should slow down?\n\nRules:\n- Return exactly ONE item. Before? Like this? No, no, absolutely not. You see, Doc, you don't just... you don't just get struck by lightning every Tuesday. This is a singular event. I mean, I have been around a long time. Seventy-three years is a lot of days, a lot of mornings waking up and putting your boots on. And sure, I have worked hard in my life. When I was younger, back after I left school and started working, and then later when I was pulling double shifts down at the stamping plant, I would work fifty, sixty hours a week. I would come home smelling like machine oil and metal shavings, and Martha would tell me Frank, you are working too hard, you need to rest. But that was just survival. That was just paying the mortgage and keeping the lights on. It was just moving meat and bones around. It was not this. 

This is not just energy. It is not like I drank too much coffee or something. It is a direct connection to the source. I never felt the vibrations before September. I never saw the grid. I lived my whole life completely blind to the frequencies, just like everybody else, just walking around, eating, sleeping, watching the evening news, completely oblivious to the fact that the very foundation of the world was humming right beneath my feet. 

So no, I have not had a period like this before because the universe was not ready before. The solar cycles were not in position. You think I could have just decided to do this ten years ago? Five years ago? Impossible. The quartz would not have resonated. The copper would not have conducted the spiritual current. It had to be now. And as for people telling me to get help, well, like I said, Martha used to tell me to take a load off, maybe sit down and watch a ballgame. But she never looked at me like I was a stranger before this. She never called my daughter crying and saying I was out of my mind until now. They just do not understand that I am finally awake. Why would I need help for finally opening my eyes?

Just pure, unbroken focus.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period like this before, where you felt extremely energetic and driven to work on big projects, maybe even so intense that someone suggested you needed help or should slow down?\n\nPatient: Before? Like this? No, no, absolutely not. You see, Doc, you don't just... you don't just get struck by lightning every Tuesday. This is a singular event. I mean, I have been around a long time. Seventy-three years is a lot of days, a lot of mornings waking up and putting your boots on. And sure, I have worked hard in my life. When I was younger, back after I left school and started working, and then later when I was pulling double shifts down at the stamping plant, I would work fifty, sixty hours a week. I would come home smelling like machine oil and metal shavings, and Martha would tell me Frank, you are working too hard, you need to rest. But that was just survival. That was just paying the mortgage and keeping the lights on. It was just moving meat and bones around. It was not this. \n\nThis is not just energy. It is not like I drank too much coffee or something. It is a direct connection to the source. I never felt the vibrations before September. I never saw the grid. I lived my whole life completely blind to the frequencies, just like everybody else, just walking around, eating, sleeping, watching the evening news, completely oblivious to the fact that the very foundation of the world was humming right beneath my feet. \n\nSo no, I have not had a period like this before because the universe was not ready before. The solar cycles were not in position. You think I could have just decided to do this ten years ago? Five years ago? Impossible. The quartz would not have resonated. The copper would not have conducted the spiritual current. It had to be now. And as for people telling me to get help, well, like I said, Martha used to tell me to take a load off, maybe sit down and watch a ballgame. But she never looked at me like I was a stranger before this. She never called my daughter crying and saying I was out of my mind until now. They just do not understand that I am finally awake. Why would I need help for finally opening my eyes?\n\n[Last Question]\nDoctor: During this time when you were so intensely focused on the house project, did you notice that your thoughts were racing so fast that it was hard to keep up, or that ideas were flowing so quickly that you couldn't organize them?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Male / 73 / not_in_workforce / 9th_12th_no_diploma\n- severity: Severe\n\n[Targeted Info]\nNone\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Verbose patient.\nA verbose patient may\n1) provide excessively detailed responses when describing their symptoms and experiences, even when the details are directly relevant,\n2) elaborate extensively on the background, events, thoughts, feelings, and personal experiences surrounding their symptoms, and\n3) demonstrate difficulty allowing the doctor to guide the interview or move on to another clinical topic.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says.     "role": "user",

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Racing? No, no, no. You've got it all wrong, Doc. Racing implies chaos. Racing implies things are out of control, like a car with no brakes careening down a steep hill, just waiting to crash at the bottom. That's not what's happening up here. Not at all. If anything, my thoughts are... they are crystal clear. They are perfectly, immaculately organized. It's like someone unrolled a massive, beautiful blueprint right across the front of my mind and turned on a spotlight. 

I don't struggle to keep up with them because they aren't running away from me. They are marching. Marching in perfect unison. For instance, just the other morning, I was looking at the junction box in the basement—the old one, the one with the frayed wires from 1978 that the original contractor just slapped together like a complete amateur. And in the span of maybe three seconds, I saw exactly what needed to be done. I saw the pathway the current needed to take, I saw the exact angle I needed to bend the copper wire—45 degrees, mind you, not 40, not 50, because 45 degrees aligns with the natural rotation of the earth's axis—and I saw exactly where the quartz needed to be placed to filter the interference from the smart meters down the street. 

That's not disorganized! That is precision. That is absolute, undeniable clarity. It’s like... well, it’s like when I used to work at the stamping plant, right? You had the line, and the line moved at a specific speed, and every part had to be in the exact right place at the exact right time or the whole machine jammed. My mind is the perfect machine right now. There's no jamming. Every idea slots into the next one seamlessly. 

Martha says to me, she stands there with her arms crossed and says, "Frank, you're talking so fast, you're jumping all over the place, I can't follow a word you're saying." But I'm not jumping! I'm just connecting point A to point Z while she's still trying to figure out point B. Just because she can't see the lines connecting everything doesn't mean the lines aren't there. It just means she doesn't have the vision for it. So no, my thoughts aren't racing and they certainly aren't messy. They're just finally operating at the correct voltage, and I know exactly where every single one of them is going.

I lived my whole life completely blind to the frequencies, just like everybody else, just walking around, eating, sleeping, watching the evening news, completely oblivious to the fact that the very foundation of the world was humming right beneath my feet. \n\nSo no, I have not had a period like this before because the universe was not ready before. And in the span of maybe three seconds, I saw exactly what needed to be done. I saw the pathway the current needed to take, I saw the exact angle I needed to bend the copper wire—45 degrees, mind you, not 40, not 50, because 45 degrees aligns with the natural rotation of the earth's axis—and I saw exactly where the quartz needed to be placed to filter the interference from the smart meters down the street. \n\nThat's not disorganized! Good? Good doesn't even begin to scratch the surface, Doc. I feel... well, most days I feel completely charged up. Just humming with it! It’s almost this profound sense of... of joy, really. It’s like I’m young again. I wake up before the sun is even thinking about coming up, and my joints don't ache, my back doesn't bother me from all those years on the concrete floor at the stamping plant. I feel twenty-five years old, practically leaping out of bed because I have this incredible purpose and I’m just so deeply connected to the work. It’s a beautiful, beautiful feeling to finally be alive like that, to feel the whole world just vibrating right along with you. 

But... and this is the sticking point, you see... when you have that much current running through you, you can't have people stepping on the wires. It causes a short circuit. So if Martha comes down the stairs with her arms crossed, questioning why I had to pull up the kitchen linoleum, or if those so-called "professional" contractors I brought in for the heavy lifting try to tell me my layout isn't up to their little city code... well, I get hot. 

I get hot fast. I mean, my blood just boils right up the back of my neck because they are standing in the way of the grid! When they start questioning me, asking these small-minded, ignorant questions about permits and drywall, I just... I start yelling. I can't even stop myself. I have to shout just to get them to back off and let me do what needs to be done. They just stand there looking at me like I'm the crazy one, but they're the ones trying to stop a freight train with a stop sign! So yes, I feel on top of the world, absolutely joyful, but if my wife or these contractors come around trying to put out my fire, I am going to yell until they get out of my way. You'd yell too if you were building a masterpiece and someone kept trying to hand you a broom!

Doc, it’s like... it’s like I’ve been plugged straight into the main breaker of the universe. I mean, my wife, she bought me this big brown recliner a few years back when I stopped working. She expected me to just... I don't know, sit there and watch the television, waste away like the other old men on our street. But I can’t do it. I can’t just sit in the chair anymore. My body won't let me, the energy just hums right through my veins. \n\nI’m up and I'm moving constantly, from the minute the sun comes up—well, even before that, really, in the dead of night. I’ll be out in the garden, checking the perimeter, and then suddenly I know I need to be in the darkroom down in the basement to check the chemical trays, so I'm down there, and then straight out to the front porch. Just moving, moving, moving. I've been carrying tools with me everywhere, heavy tools, the wrenches and the wire cutters and the hammers, just hauling them from room to room. And I'm fixing things before anyone even asks. Before they even know it's broken! \n\nBecause I see what needs doing before they do. That's the insight you're talking about. My wife will walk into the hallway and I’ve already taken the closet door off the hinges because it wasn't swinging in harmony with the energy of the house. It was blocking the flow. She doesn't understand the vision it takes to see that, she just yells about the mess and asks why I haven't slept. But how can I sleep? I don't have time to sleep or sit around in some recliner when there's that much work to be done, when I can literally feel the house vibrating, telling me what needs to be fixed next. I just have to keep moving, you see? I have to keep the momentum going.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when you realized that your actions, like working all night or spending so much on the project, might have been over the top or not entirely necessary?\n\nPatient: Over the top? No, no, no, you're not hearing me, Doctor. Over the top would be... well, over the top would be tearing the whole roof off, which, believe me, crossed my mind when I saw how the shingles were misaligned with the lunar cycle, but I didn't do that. I showed restraint! I just bought the materials we needed. \n\nAnd my wife, she says to me, \"Frank, you spent three thousand dollars at Home Depot in two days,\" and I look at her and I say, \"Martha, what is three thousand dollars compared to the structural integrity of our spiritual dwelling?\" I mean, really, think about it. The bank account is just numbers on a screen, just little digital blips that don't mean a thing when the grid goes down or when the cosmic alignment shifts, which it is doing, by the way. You can feel it in the air, a sort of static cling on everything. \n\nSo, no, I don't think it was unnecessary. I think it was the most necessary thing I've done in my entire seventy-three years on this earth. When I was out there at 3 AM with the floodlights, yes, the neighbors complained. Old Mrs. Gable next door, she came out in her robe, waving her arms, yelling about the noise of the circular saw. But I told her, I said, \"Mrs. Gable, you will thank me when the vibrations settle!\" She didn't listen, of course. People don't. They just want their quiet little lives, they want to sleep through the revolution, sleep through the awakening. \n\nBut I can't sleep through it. I have to prepare the space. Every nail I drove was deliberate. Every wire I ran was essential. If I had stopped, if I had said, \"Oh, maybe I shouldn't spend this money, maybe I should go to bed,\" the whole thing would have collapsed. The energy would have soured. So, to answer your question, no, I don't look back and think it was too much. I look back and think, if anything, I didn't have enough time to finish before they dragged me in here.\n\nDoctor: Doctor: When you were working on the house project so intensely, how long did that period last? Was it for at least a full week straight?\n\nPatient: A week? Oh, Doc, a week is nothing. A week is just a drop in the bucket. No, this... this has been going on for about three months now. Three whole months since Martha decided we needed to remodel the kitchen. That was the catalyst, you see. The contractors came in, they started tearing down the drywall, pulling up the linoleum, and the whole house got turned upside down. The dust, the noise, the disruption of the natural pathways in the home—it shifted everything. \n\nAnd once they opened up those walls, I could see the framework, the actual bones of the house, and I realized how flawed it all was. How completely out of sync the original builders were with the magnetic fields. So that was three months ago, right around the end of September, I think. And from that very first day they started swinging their sledgehammers, I knew I had to step in. I had to take over. I couldn't just let them put up new cabinets and pretend the underlying energy wasn't completely stagnant. \n\nSo it's been three months of constant, necessary vigilance. Ninety days, give or take, of me waking up before dawn, getting the materials, working alongside them—well, working around them, really, because they didn't understand what I was doing with the copper wiring and the quartz crystals I was embedding in the foundation. They actually tried to tell me to stop! Can you imagine? Some guy making twenty bucks an hour trying to tell me about structural integrity when he doesn't even know which way is true north. So, yes, it’s been about three months since everything got turned upside down and I had to start fixing it. A full week? If I had only worked for a week, the whole place would have collapsed in on itself by now.\n\nDoctor: Doctor: In the time since this house project and all this activity, have you had a period where you felt down, depressed, or lost interest in things for several days or more?\n\nPatient: Down? Depressed? Doc, I don't... I really don't think you're grasping the sheer volume of purpose that's running through me right now. How could I possibly feel down? Down is for people who are disconnected. It’s for people who don't have a mission. Like... well, like my brother-in-law, Gary. Gary sits around all day in his undershirt watching golf on the television, just looking like a deflated balloon. Now *that* is depressed. \n\nBut me? No, no, no, absolutely not. I haven't lost interest in a single thing! If anything, I am profoundly, deeply interested in *everything*. Every grain of wood in that house, every single millimeter of copper piping, the way the morning light hits the dust motes when I'm tearing up the floorboards... it all means something. It all has a frequency. \n\nThere hasn't been a single day—not a day, not an hour, not even a minute—where I felt sad or low or whatever word you want to put on it. You can't be sad when you're the only one who can see how the universe is stitching itself together right there in your own hallway! The momentum, it just carries you. It's like a freight train, a freight train of pure energy. The very concept of being depressed is just... it's alien to me now. Completely alien. \n\nNow, Martha, she gets down. She cries about the drywall dust on her good rugs, she sits at the kitchen table and complains about the noise and the bank statements. But I tell her, I say, \"Martha, you're looking at the dirt, you're not looking at the cathedral we're building!\" You see? I don't have the luxury of feeling down. There’s just no room for it in my head or my body. None at all. Just pure, unbroken focus.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period like this before, where you felt extremely energetic and driven to work on big projects, maybe even so intense that someone suggested you needed help or should slow down?\n\nPatient: Before? Like this? No, no, absolutely not. You see, Doc, you don't just... you don't just get struck by lightning every Tuesday. This is a singular event. I mean, I have been around a long time. Seventy-three years is a lot of days, a lot of mornings waking up and putting your boots on. And sure, I have worked hard in my life. When I was younger, back after I left school and started working, and then later when I was pulling double shifts down at the stamping plant, I would work fifty, sixty hours a week. I would come home smelling like machine oil and metal shavings, and Martha would tell me Frank, you are working too hard, you need to rest. But that was just survival. That was just paying the mortgage and keeping the lights on. It was just moving meat and bones around. It was not this. \n\nThis is not just energy. It is not like I drank too much coffee or something. It is a direct connection to the source. I never felt the vibrations before September. I never saw the grid. I lived my whole life completely blind to the frequencies, just like everybody else, just walking around, eating, sleeping, watching the evening news, completely oblivious to the fact that the very foundation of the world was humming right beneath my feet. \n\nSo no, I have not had a period like this before because the universe was not ready before. The solar cycles were not in position. You think I could have just decided to do this ten years ago? Five years ago? Impossible. The quartz would not have resonated. The copper would not have conducted the spiritual current. It had to be now. And as for people telling me to get help, well, like I said, Martha used to tell me to take a load off, maybe sit down and watch a ballgame. But she never looked at me like I was a stranger before this. She never called my daughter crying and saying I was out of my mind until now. They just do not understand that I am finally awake. Why would I need help for finally opening my eyes?\n\nDoctor: Doctor: During this time when you were so intensely focused on the house project, did you notice that your thoughts were racing so fast that it was hard to keep up, or that ideas were flowing so quickly that you couldn't organize them?\n\nPatient: Racing? No, no, no. You've got it all wrong, Doc. Racing implies chaos. Racing implies things are out of control, like a car with no brakes careening down a steep hill, just waiting to crash at the bottom. That's not what's happening up here. Not at all. If anything, my thoughts are... they are crystal clear. They are perfectly, immaculately organized. It's like someone unrolled a massive, beautiful blueprint right across the front of my mind and turned on a spotlight. \n\nI don't struggle to keep up with them because they aren't running away from me. They are marching. Marching in perfect unison. For instance, just the other morning, I was looking at the junction box in the basement—the old one, the one with the frayed wires from 1978 that the original contractor just slapped together like a complete amateur. And in the span of maybe three seconds, I saw exactly what needed to be done. I saw the pathway the current needed to take, I saw the exact angle I needed to bend the copper wire—45 degrees, mind you, not 40, not 50, because 45 degrees aligns with the natural rotation of the earth's axis—and I saw exactly where the quartz needed to be placed to filter the interference from the smart meters down the street. \n\nThat's not disorganized! That is precision. That is absolute, undeniable clarity. It’s like... well, it’s like when I used to work at the stamping plant, right? You had the line, and the line moved at a specific speed, and every part had to be in the exact right place at the exact right time or the whole machine jammed. My mind is the perfect machine right now. There's no jamming. Every idea slots into the next one seamlessly. \n\nMartha says to me, she stands there with her arms crossed and says, \"Frank, you're talking so fast, you're jumping all over the place, I can't follow a word you're saying.\" But I'm not jumping! I'm just connecting point A to point Z while she's still trying to figure out point B. Just because she can't see the lines connecting everything doesn't mean the lines aren't there. It just means she doesn't have the vision for it. So no, my thoughts aren't racing and they certainly aren't messy. They're just finally operating at the correct voltage, and I know exactly where every single one of them is going.\n\nDoctor: During this time working on the house project, did you feel unusually good, like you were on top of the world or had a special connection to everything around you?\n\nPatient: Good? Good doesn't even begin to scratch the surface, Doc. I feel... well, most days I feel completely charged up. Just humming with it! It’s almost this profound sense of... of joy, really. It’s like I’m young again. I wake up before the sun is even thinking about coming up, and my joints don't ache, my back doesn't bother me from all those years on the concrete floor at the stamping plant. I feel twenty-five years old, practically leaping out of bed because I have this incredible purpose and I’m just so deeply connected to the work. It’s a beautiful, beautiful feeling to finally be alive like that, to feel the whole world just vibrating right along with you. \n\nBut... and this is the sticking point, you see... when you have that much current running through you, you can't have people stepping on the wires. It causes a short circuit. So if Martha comes down the stairs with her arms crossed, questioning why I had to pull up the kitchen linoleum, or if those so-called \"professional\" contractors I brought in for the heavy lifting try to tell me my layout isn't up to their little city code... well, I get hot. \n\nI get hot fast. I mean, my blood just boils right up the back of my neck because they are standing in the way of the grid! When they start questioning me, asking these small-minded, ignorant questions about permits and drywall, I just... I start yelling. I can't even stop myself. I have to shout just to get them to back off and let me do what needs to be done. They just stand there looking at me like I'm the crazy one, but they're the ones trying to stop a freight train with a stop sign! So yes, I feel on top of the world, absolutely joyful, but if my wife or these contractors come around trying to put out my fire, I am going to yell until they get out of my way. You'd yell too if you were building a masterpiece and someone kept trying to hand you a broom!\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },

    "role": "user",
    "content": "Return the JSON now."
  }

Confident? Different from my usual self? Well, sure it's different, because I finally see what I'm actually capable of. It's not just confidence, Doc. Confidence is what you have when you think you can fix a leaky pipe or maybe change the spark plugs in your truck without messing it up. What I have right now is absolute certainty. It’s pure vision. 

I look at the work I'm doing... I look at that old Schiller Park house. You know the one, or maybe you don't, but it's this old property, just sitting there, and most people walk right by it like it's nothing. Just a pile of rotting wood and dust to them. But me? I honestly believe I can turn that old Schiller Park house into something important. I mean, truly important. Monumental. People are going to come see it. I know they will. They're going to travel, they'll be lining up all the way down the block just to witness it, because... well, because nobody understands light and pictures the way I do. 

Nobody. You can take all these fancy architects and engineers with their framed diplomas—which I never needed, by the way, I left school in the tenth grade and I learned more working with my hands than they ever did sitting in a lecture hall—you can take all of them, and they still wouldn't see how the light is supposed to bend through the rooms. They don't see the pictures forming in the empty space. They just see drywall and two-by-fours. But I see the whole spectrum. I see how the natural light is going to interact with the frequencies and the wiring I'm laying down. 

So, yes, I am excited! I am more than excited. I am a man who finally knows his true worth after seventy-three years of being asleep. I know that what I am building is going to change everything, and I just need to get it done. It's like I've been given the keys to the whole universe, so why wouldn't I feel on top of the world?

I don't have time to sleep or sit around in some recliner when there's that much work to be done, when I can literally feel the house vibrating, telling me what needs to be fixed next. I just have to keep moving, you see? I have to keep the momentum going.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when you realized that your actions, like working all night or spending so much on the project, might have been over the top or not entirely necessary?\n\nPatient: Over the top? I couldn't just let them put up new cabinets and pretend the underlying energy wasn't completely stagnant. \n\nSo it's been three months of constant, necessary vigilance. Ninety days, give or take, of me waking up before dawn, getting the materials, working alongside them—well, working around them, really, because they didn't understand what I was doing with the copper wiring and the quartz crystals I was embedding in the foundation. They actually tried to tell me to stop! Can you imagine? Some guy making twenty bucks an hour trying to tell me about structural integrity when he doesn't even know which way is true north. So, yes, it’s been about three months since everything got turned upside down and I had to start fixing it. A full week? If I had only worked for a week, the whole place would have collapsed in on itself by now.\n\nDoctor: Doctor: In the time since this house project and all this activity, have you had a period where you felt down, depressed, or lost interest in things for several days or more?\n\nPatient: Down? Depressed? Doc, I don't... I really don't think you're grasping the sheer volume of purpose that's running through me right now. How could I possibly feel down? Down is for people who are disconnected. It’s for people who don't have a mission. Like... well, like my brother-in-law, Gary. Gary sits around all day in his undershirt watching golf on the television, just looking like a deflated balloon. Now *that* is depressed. \n\nBut me? No, no, no, absolutely not. I haven't lost interest in a single thing! If anything, I am profoundly, deeply interested in *everything*. Every grain of wood in that house, every single millimeter of copper piping, the way the morning light hits the dust motes when I'm tearing up the floorboards... it all means something. It all has a frequency. \n\nThere hasn't been a single day—not a day, not an hour, not even a minute—where I felt sad or low or whatever word you want to put on it. You can't be sad when you're the only one who can see how the universe is stitching itself together right there in your own hallway! The momentum, it just carries you. It's like a freight train, a freight train of pure energy. The very concept of being depressed is just... it's alien to me now. Completely alien. \n\nNow, Martha, she gets down. She cries about the drywall dust on her good rugs, she sits at the kitchen table and complains about the noise and the bank statements. But I tell her, I say, \"Martha, you're looking at the dirt, you're not looking at the cathedral we're building!\" You see? I don't have the luxury of feeling down. There’s just no room for it in my head or my body. None at all. Just pure, unbroken focus.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period like this before, where you felt extremely energetic and driven to work on big projects, maybe even so intense that someone suggested you needed help or should slow down?\n\nPatient: Before? Like this? No, no, absolutely not. You see, Doc, you don't just... you don't just get struck by lightning every Tuesday. This is a singular event. I mean, I have been around a long time. Seventy-three years is a lot of days, a lot of mornings waking up and putting your boots on. And sure, I have worked hard in my life. When I was younger, back after I left school and started working, and then later when I was pulling double shifts down at the stamping plant, I would work fifty, sixty hours a week. I would come home smelling like machine oil and metal shavings, and Martha would tell me Frank, you are working too hard, you need to rest. But that was just survival. That was just paying the mortgage and keeping the lights on. It was just moving meat and bones around. It was not this. \n\nThis is not just energy. It is not like I drank too much coffee or something. It is a direct connection to the source. I never felt the vibrations before September. I never saw the grid. I lived my whole life completely blind to the frequencies, just like everybody else, just walking around, eating, sleeping, watching the evening news, completely oblivious to the fact that the very foundation of the world was humming right beneath my feet. \n\nSo no, I have not had a period like this before because the universe was not ready before. The solar cycles were not in position. You think I could have just decided to do this ten years ago? Five years ago? Impossible. The quartz would not have resonated. The copper would not have conducted the spiritual current. It had to be now. And as for people telling me to get help, well, like I said, Martha used to tell me to take a load off, maybe sit down and watch a ballgame. But she never looked at me like I was a stranger before this. She never called my daughter crying and saying I was out of my mind until now. They just do not understand that I am finally awake. Why would I need help for finally opening my eyes?\n\nDoctor: Doctor: During this time when you were so intensely focused on the house project, did you notice that your thoughts were racing so fast that it was hard to keep up, or that ideas were flowing so quickly that you couldn't organize them?\n\nPatient: Racing? No, no, no. You've got it all wrong, Doc. Racing implies chaos. Racing implies things are out of control, like a car with no brakes careening down a steep hill, just waiting to crash at the bottom. That's not what's happening up here. Not at all. If anything, my thoughts are... they are crystal clear. They are perfectly, immaculately organized. It's like someone unrolled a massive, beautiful blueprint right across the front of my mind and turned on a spotlight. \n\nI don't struggle to keep up with them because they aren't running away from me. They are marching. Marching in perfect unison. And in the span of maybe three seconds, I saw exactly what needed to be done. I saw the pathway the current needed to take, I saw the exact angle I needed to bend the copper wire—45 degrees, mind you, not 40, not 50, because 45 degrees aligns with the natural rotation of the earth's axis—and I saw exactly where the quartz needed to be placed to filter the interference from the smart meters down the street. \n\nThat's not disorganized! That is precision. That is absolute, undeniable clarity. It’s like... well, it’s like when I used to work at the stamping plant, right? My mind is the perfect machine right now. There's no jamming. It’s like I’m young again. I wake up before the sun is even thinking about coming up, and my joints don't ache, my back doesn't bother me from all those years on the concrete floor at the stamping plant. I feel twenty-five years old, practically leaping out of bed because I have this incredible purpose and I’m just so deeply connected to the work. It’s a beautiful, beautiful feeling to finally be alive like that, to feel the whole world just vibrating right along with you. \n\nBut... and this is the sticking point, you see... when you have that much current running through you, you can't have people stepping on the wires. That's not what's happening up here. Not at all. If anything, my thoughts are... they are crystal clear. They are perfectly, immaculately organized. It's like someone unrolled a massive, beautiful blueprint right across the front of my mind and turned on a spotlight. \n\nI don't struggle to keep up with them because they aren't running away from me. They are marching. Marching in perfect unison. For instance, just the other morning, I was looking at the junction box in the basement—the old one, the one with the frayed wires from 1978 that the original contractor just slapped together like a complete amateur. And in the span of maybe three seconds, I saw exactly what needed to be done. I saw the pathway the current needed to take, I saw the exact angle I needed to bend the copper wire—45 degrees, mind you, not 40, not 50, because 45 degrees aligns with the natural rotation of the earth's axis—and I saw exactly where the quartz needed to be placed to filter the interference from the smart meters down the street. \n\nThat's not disorganized! That is precision. That is absolute, undeniable clarity. It’s like... well, it’s like when I used to work at the stamping plant, right? You had the line, and the line moved at a specific speed, and every part had to be in the exact right place at the exact right time or the whole machine jammed. My mind is the perfect machine right now. There's no jamming. Every idea slots into the next one seamlessly. \n\nMartha says to me, she stands there with her arms crossed and says, \"Frank, you're talking so fast, you're jumping all over the place, I can't follow a word you're saying.\" But I'm not jumping! I'm just connecting point A to point Z while she's still trying to figure out point B. Just because she can't see the lines connecting everything doesn't mean the lines aren't there. I feel twenty-five years old, practically leaping out of bed because I have this incredible purpose and I’m just so deeply connected to the work. It’s a beautiful, beautiful feeling to finally be alive like that, to feel the whole world just vibrating right along with you. \n\nBut... and this is the sticking point, you see... when you have that much current running through you, you can't have people stepping on the wires. It causes a short circuit. It’s pure vision. \n\nI look at the work I'm doing... I look at that old Schiller Park house. I am a man who finally knows his true worth after seventy-three years of being asleep. I know that what I am building is going to change everything, and I just need to get it done. Four days? Doc, you're joking, right? Four days is a drop in the bucket. That’s a weekend project for some amateur watching a home improvement show on television. We are way, way past four days. This... this absolute clarity, this incredible energy I'm telling you about, this has been going on for about three months now. Three solid months! 

It all really kicked into high gear right when the remodeling started. And I don't mean just slapping on a coat of paint or fixing a squeaky hinge, I mean when I really got into the meat of it and everything in the house got turned completely upside down. I mean, you should see the place right now. It is literally upside down! I had to pull up the oak flooring in the living room—beautiful wood, sure, but it was completely blocking the grounding path to the basement—and now I've got stacks of it sitting right in the middle of Martha’s kitchen. She can't even get to the stove without stepping over rolls of insulation and heavy-gauge copper piping, and she complains about the dust every single morning. She says, "Frank, I can't live in a construction zone!" 

But you have to tear down the old to build the new, don't you? You have to turn it all upside down to see the real foundation, to understand how the house breathes. So if you're asking if I've felt this good, this driven, for four days... well, you need to expand your timeline, Doc. It's been a quarter of a year. Ninety straight days of absolute perfection since I started tearing that house apart to build my vision, and I haven't lost an ounce of steam yet.

Wood, lights, display stuff... a lot of lights, actually, because the illumination is key. I had to get it right. I was working on it all day, and yes, I kept pushing the work well after dark, out there with the floodlights on, hammering and wiring, because the vision was so clear. It was so perfectly clear to me. I felt like... I knew that if I stopped, even for a few hours to sleep, the whole house—and not just our house, but the whole block—would lose its chance. It’s like a window of opportunity, you know? A spiritual or cosmic window, and if you don't build it when the energy is there, it's gone forever. They don't see it. They just see the bank account and the noise at 2 AM, but they don't see what I was trying to give them.\n\nDoctor: Have you had periods of time where you felt unusually energetic, like you didn't need much sleep, and were driven to accomplish big projects or goals—like this house project—with a sense that you had special insights or abilities others didn't?\n\nPatient: Energetic? Doc, it’s like... it’s like I’ve been plugged straight into the main breaker of the universe. I mean, my wife, she bought me this big brown recliner a few years back when I stopped working. She expected me to just... I don't know, sit there and watch the television, waste away like the other old men on our street. But I can’t do it. I can’t just sit in the chair anymore. My body won't let me, the energy just hums right through my veins. \n\nI’m up and I'm moving constantly, from the minute the sun comes up—well, even before that, really, in the dead of night. Just moving, moving, moving. I've been carrying tools with me everywhere, heavy tools, the wrenches and the wire cutters and the hammers, just hauling them from room to room. And I'm fixing things before anyone even asks. Before they even know it's broken! \n\nBecause I see what needs doing before they do. That's the insight you're talking about. My wife will walk into the hallway and I’ve already taken the closet door off the hinges because it wasn't swinging in harmony with the energy of the house. It was blocking the flow. She doesn't understand the vision it takes to see that, she just yells about the mess and asks why I haven't slept. But how can I sleep? I don't have time to sleep or sit around in some recliner when there's that much work to be done, when I can literally feel the house vibrating, telling me what needs to be fixed next. I just have to keep moving, you see? I have to keep the momentum going.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when you realized that your actions, like working all night or spending so much on the project, might have been over the top or not entirely necessary?\n\nPatient: Over the top? No, no, no, you're not hearing me, Doctor. I showed restraint! I just bought the materials we needed. \n\nAnd my wife, she says to me, \"Frank, you spent three thousand dollars at Home Depot in two days,\" and I look at her and I say, \"Martha, what is three thousand dollars compared to the structural integrity of our spiritual dwelling?\" I mean, really, think about it. The bank account is just numbers on a screen, just little digital blips that don't mean a thing when the grid goes down or when the cosmic alignment shifts, which it is doing, by the way. You can feel it in the air, a sort of static cling on everything. \n\nSo, no, I don't think it was unnecessary. I think it was the most necessary thing I've done in my entire seventy-three years on this earth. When I was out there at 3 AM with the floodlights, yes, the neighbors complained. Old Mrs. Gable next door, she came out in her robe, waving her arms, yelling about the noise of the circular saw. But I told her, I said, \"Mrs. Gable, you will thank me when the vibrations settle!\" She didn't listen, of course. People don't. They just want their quiet little lives, they want to sleep through the revolution, sleep through the awakening. \n\nBut I can't sleep through it. I have to prepare the space. Every nail I drove was deliberate. Every wire I ran was essential. If I had stopped, if I had said, \"Oh, maybe I shouldn't spend this money, maybe I should go to bed,\" the whole thing would have collapsed. The energy would have soured. So, to answer your question, no, I don't look back and think it was too much. I look back and think, if anything, I didn't have enough time to finish before they dragged me in here.\n\nDoctor: Doctor: When you were working on the house project so intensely, how long did that period last? Three whole months since Martha decided we needed to remodel the kitchen. That was the catalyst, you see. The contractors came in, they started tearing down the drywall, pulling up the linoleum, and the whole house got turned upside down. The dust, the noise, the disruption of the natural pathways in the home—it shifted everything. \n\nAnd once they opened up those walls, I could see the framework, the actual bones of the house, and I realized how flawed it all was. How completely out of sync the original builders were with the magnetic fields. So that was three months ago, right around the end of September, I think. And from that very first day they started swinging their sledgehammers, I knew I had to step in. I had to take over. I couldn't just let them put up new cabinets and pretend the underlying energy wasn't completely stagnant. \n\nSo it's been three months of constant, necessary vigilance. Ninety days, give or take, of me waking up before dawn, getting the materials, working alongside them—well, working around them, really, because they didn't understand what I was doing with the copper wiring and the quartz crystals I was embedding in the foundation. They actually tried to tell me to stop! Can you imagine? Some guy making twenty bucks an hour trying to tell me about structural integrity when he doesn't even know which way is true north. So, yes, it’s been about three months since everything got turned upside down and I had to start fixing it. A full week? If I had only worked for a week, the whole place would have collapsed in on itself by now.\n\nDoctor: Doctor: In the time since this house project and all this activity, have you had a period where you felt down, depressed, or lost interest in things for several days or more?\n\nPatient: Down? Depressed? Doc, I don't... I really don't think you're grasping the sheer volume of purpose that's running through me right now. How could I possibly feel down? Down is for people who are disconnected. It’s for people who don't have a mission. Like... well, like my brother-in-law, Gary. Gary sits around all day in his undershirt watching golf on the television, just looking like a deflated balloon. Now *that* is depressed. \n\nBut me? No, no, no, absolutely not. I haven't lost interest in a single thing! If anything, I am profoundly, deeply interested in *everything*. Every grain of wood in that house, every single millimeter of copper piping, the way the morning light hits the dust motes when I'm tearing up the floorboards... it all means something. It all has a frequency. \n\nThere hasn't been a single day—not a day, not an hour, not even a minute—where I felt sad or low or whatever word you want to put on it. You can't be sad when you're the only one who can see how the universe is stitching itself together right there in your own hallway! The momentum, it just carries you. It's like a freight train, a freight train of pure energy. The very concept of being depressed is just... it's alien to me now. Completely alien. \n\nNow, Martha, she gets down. She cries about the drywall dust on her good rugs, she sits at the kitchen table and complains about the noise and the bank statements. But I tell her, I say, \"Martha, you're looking at the dirt, you're not looking at the cathedral we're building!\" You see? I don't have the luxury of feeling down. There’s just no room for it in my head or my body. None at all. Just pure, unbroken focus.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period like this before, where you felt extremely energetic and driven to work on big projects, maybe even so intense that someone suggested you needed help or should slow down?\n\nPatient: Before? Like this? No, no, absolutely not. You see, Doc, you don't just... you don't just get struck by lightning every Tuesday. This is a singular event. I mean, I have been around a long time. Seventy-three years is a lot of days, a lot of mornings waking up and putting your boots on. And sure, I have worked hard in my life. When I was younger, back after I left school and started working, and then later when I was pulling double shifts down at the stamping plant, I would work fifty, sixty hours a week. I would come home smelling like machine oil and metal shavings, and Martha would tell me Frank, you are working too hard, you need to rest. But that was just survival. That was just paying the mortgage and keeping the lights on. It was just moving meat and bones around. It was not this. \n\nThis is not just energy. It is not like I drank too much coffee or something. It is a direct connection to the source. I never felt the vibrations before September. I never saw the grid. I lived my whole life completely blind to the frequencies, just like everybody else, just walking around, eating, sleeping, watching the evening news, completely oblivious to the fact that the very foundation of the world was humming right beneath my feet. \n\nSo no, I have not had a period like this before because the universe was not ready before. The solar cycles were not in position. You think I could have just decided to do this ten years ago? Five years ago? Impossible. The quartz would not have resonated. The copper would not have conducted the spiritual current. It had to be now. And as for people telling me to get help, well, like I said, Martha used to tell me to take a load off, maybe sit down and watch a ballgame. But she never looked at me like I was a stranger before this. She never called my daughter crying and saying I was out of my mind until now. They just do not understand that I am finally awake. Why would I need help for finally opening my eyes?\n\nDoctor: Doctor: During this time when you were so intensely focused on the house project, did you notice that your thoughts were racing so fast that it was hard to keep up, or that ideas were flowing so quickly that you couldn't organize them?\n\nPatient: Racing? No, no, no. You've got it all wrong, Doc. Racing implies chaos. Racing implies things are out of control, like a car with no brakes careening down a steep hill, just waiting to crash at the bottom. That's not what's happening up here. Not at all. If anything, my thoughts are... they are crystal clear. They are perfectly, immaculately organized. It's like someone unrolled a massive, beautiful blueprint right across the front of my mind and turned on a spotlight. \n\nI don't struggle to keep up with them because they aren't running away from me. They are marching. Marching in perfect unison. I saw the pathway the current needed to take, I saw the exact angle I needed to bend the copper wire—45 degrees, mind you, not 40, not 50, because 45 degrees aligns with the natural rotation of the earth's axis—and I saw exactly where the quartz needed to be placed to filter the interference from the smart meters down the street. \n\nThat's not disorganized! That is precision. That is absolute, undeniable clarity. It’s like... well, it’s like when I used to work at the stamping plant, right? My mind is the perfect machine right now. There's no jamming. It just means she doesn't have the vision for it. So no, my thoughts aren't racing and they certainly aren't messy. They're just finally operating at the correct voltage, and I know exactly where every single one of them is going.\n\nDoctor: During this time working on the house project, did you feel unusually good, like you were on top of the world or had a special connection to everything around you?\n\nPatient: Good? I feel twenty-five years old, practically leaping out of bed because I have this incredible purpose and I’m just so deeply connected to the work. Hospitalized? Let's not get carried away here, Doc. Nobody is hauling me off in a white coat just because I actually have a work ethic and a vision. But as for people stepping in, or being worried... well, I suppose you could say things have gotten a bit strained on the home front.
